- `1 incrementally so we can get it all addressed.` 

- `2 So in regards to that -- and I appreciate the` 

- `3 position of the parties.  It is important for me to hear all` 

- `4 sides.  Article 1, Section 28 of the Utah Constitution` 

- `5 recognizes important rights afforded to victims and victims'` 

- `6 representatives, including the right to be treated with` 

- `7 fairness, respect, and dignity and the right to be present and` 

- `8 heard at critical stages of the proceedings.  The Court` 

- `9 acknowledges the concerns raised in the motion regarding` 

- `10 transparency and the ability to meaningfully observe the` 

- `11 evidence presented during this hearing.  At the same time, the` 

- `12 Court has an independent obligation to manage these proceedings` 

- `13 in a manner that safeguards the rights of all parties.  That` 

- `14 includes honoring the rights of victims and victims' 15 representatives while preserving the defendant's constitutional` 

- `16 rights, including the right to a fair and impartial process.` 

- `17 The Court has considered the request that exhibits be` 

- `18 displayed in realtime to the courtroom.  After careful 19 consideration, the Court determines that not all exhibits will 20 be visually displayed to the gallery and notes that it will use 21 a tiered method as described, first whether it will be admitted 22 to the Court for consideration as it relates to probable cause;` 

- `23 second, whether it will be displayed only to the gallery; and` 

- `24 third, if it will be published through the camera to the public 25 at large.`

- `4 attributes to Mr. Robinson.  Attorneys for Mr. Robinson argue`

- `11 for the purposes of preliminary hearing while limiting their` 

- `12 public display or broadcast.`

- `15 argues that the exhibits are materially different from the` 

- `16 televised custodial interrogation at issue in Rideau because` 

- `17 they are written communications allegedly made outside law` 

- `18 enforcement custody.  The state also argues that the potential` 

- `19 prejudice can be addressed through trial management measures` 

- `20 including voir dire and that the victim representative has an` 

- `21 interest in being present for and able to follow important 22 criminal proceedings.` 

- `23 The Court has considered the parties' arguments, the` 

- `24 nature of the exhibits, and the procedural posture of this` 

- `25 preliminary hearing.  The defendant's due process interests,`

NOTEWORTHYREPORTING,LLC

35
```

- `1 the public nature of the criminal proceedings, and the` 

- `2 interests asserted by the state and victim representative.  On` 

- `3 the present record, the Court is not persuaded that the` 

- `4 publication of Exhibits 16.1, 16.2, and 16.3 during the` 

- `5 preliminary hearing creates the type of inherent or presumed` 

- `6 prejudice that requires categorical restrictions under Rideau,` 

- `7 Estes, and Skilling.` 

- `8 Unlike the broadcast interrogation in Rideau, the` 

- `9 exhibits at issue are written communications allegedly made` 

- `10 outside police custody and are being offered in the ordinary` 

- `11 course of a judicial proceeding.  That distinction does not` 

- `12 eliminate the possibility of prejudice, but it weighs against` 

- `13 treating publication of the exhibits as presumptively 14 unconstitutional.` 

- `15 The Court further finds that any potential` 

- `16 prejudice arising from media coverage may be addressed if` 

- `17 necessary through the appropriate trial procedures including` 

- `18 voir dire and any other measures warranted before trial.  This` 

- `19 ruling is limited to publication of the exhibits during the 20 preliminary hearing and does not determine their admissibility 21 at trial -- and I insert if one occurs -- or foreclose any` 

- `22 later request for release -- relief based on a more developed 23 record.`

- `1 action and an auto-ejecting gun?` 

- `2` **`A.`** `Yeah.  A bolt action is one where the user actually` 

- `3 has to manipulate the bolt, so they would actually have to` 

- `4 turn -- they would actually have to pull it back and forward` 

- `5 to -- whether it be to load it, lock it, as well as extract` 

- `6 anything that's in it.  Unlike a semi-auto gun, that is one --` 

- `7 every single time you pull the trigger, a bullet will be fired.`

- `9 a new cartridge will then be automatically fed into the` 

- `10 chamber.`

- `12 shell casing automatically exit the gun?`

- `16 large group of items that characterize them together.  So for a` 

- `17 bullet, you can think of the diameter of the bullet, the number` 

- `18 of land and grooves, the direction of twists, and the type of 19 rifling.` 

- `20 So I would go through and look at the class` 

- `21 characteristics of this bullet jacket fragment, which was 6` 

- `22 right conventional rifling.  And then I move on to look at any` 

- `23 subclass characteristics.  And subclass characteristics are` 

- `24 more restrictive than class but less than individual, and they` 

- `25 are design features that can be put on an object during the`

```

- `1 manufacturing process, which is consistent among items that are` 

- `2 made by a tool that's in a specific state of wear.` 

- `3 So what I mean by that is it's going to be a subset` 

- `4 of a larger group, which is why it's not used for -- excuse` 

- `5 me -- for identification or comparison purposes for the basis` 

- `6 of a conclusion.  But when you think of something that's made` 

- `7 through a mold process, when that mold comes together, it` 

- `8 creates a seam line, and that seam line is going to be` 

- `9 represented on all the items that it makes.  So that's why you` 

- `10 can't use it for the identification process, because it's going` 

- `11 to be on a lot of items, which is why it's a subset of the` 

- `12 group.` 

- `13 Then I move on at looking at the individual` 

- `14 characteristics, and these are imperfections and irregularities` 

- `15 that arise from the manufacturing process.  And then there's` 

- `16 also -- individual characteristics can arise from wear, use and` 

- `17 abuse of a tool.  And these individual characteristics is what` 

- `18 is going to be distinct to that tool, and that's why they're` 

- `19 used for the identification process.`
